I wasn’t sure if I should mention it, because I don’t want you to think I’m making things up or being foolish.\n\nOn a few nights, I heard my mother’s voice—she passed away—telling me where to put the marigolds and the candles. It felt very real at the time. And once, I thought I saw her standing near the kitchen doorway, but my husband said there was no one there.\n\nI know that sounds strange, doctor. I’m not trying to be difficult… I just thought I should be honest since you asked.\n\nDoctor: Apart from hearing your mother’s voice or seeing her, have you experienced any other unusual thoughts, visions, or voices that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Not really visions or voices apart from that… I mean, the main thing is what I said about my in-laws. I know it may sound like I’m taking it too personally, and maybe I’m not explaining it well, but I felt sure they weren’t just arguing with me.\n\nIt felt like they were using the altar, and the parish committee people, to push me out… to make me disappear from the family’s place, almost, and make everyone forget what I’ve done for them. I’m sorry if that sounds strange. I’m just trying to answer honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel that others are acting against you or plotting to harm you, even when others don’t share that belief?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say “often” with everyone, doctor… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But with my in-laws, yes, I did feel that way.\n\nThat’s really why I came in. I knew they weren’t just arguing with me. It felt like they were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out… and make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family. I know that may sound like a lot, but that’s how it felt to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that people outside of your in-laws were also acting against you or trying to harm you in some way?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think it was really people outside the family, doctor. At least, not in the same way.\n\nIt was mostly my in-laws. I came in because I knew it wasn’t just normal arguing with me. It felt like they were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out, and to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family. I know that may sound dramatic… I’m sorry. But that’s what I believed was happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any beliefs or ideas that other people close to you say are not true, but you still feel certain about them even though there’s no clear proof?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose that would be the thing with my in-laws. I don’t want to sound unreasonable, doctor, and maybe other people didn’t see it the way I did, but I felt certain they weren’t just arguing with me.\n\nI believed they were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out, like they wanted to erase my place in the family and make everyone forget what I’ve done for them. Like they wanted me out of the family’s place in things… and wanted everyone to forget what I’ve done for them. I know that may sound strange, but that’s honestly why I came in.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you’re seeing or hearing things that others don’t, or had thoughts that you realize might not be true but feel very real to you?\n\nPatient: I… yes, a little. I wasn’t sure if I should mention it, because I don’t want you to think I’m making things up or being foolish.\n\nOn a few nights, I heard my mother’s voice—she passed away—telling me where to put the marigolds and the candles. It felt very real at the time. And once, I thought I saw her standing near the kitchen doorway, but my husband said there was no one there.\n\nI know that sounds strange, doctor. I’m not trying to be difficult… I just thought I should be honest since you asked.\n\nDoctor: Apart from hearing your mother’s voice or seeing her, have you experienced any other unusual thoughts, visions, or voices that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Not really visions or voices apart from that… I mean, the main thing is what I said about my in-laws. I know it may sound like I’m taking it too personally, and maybe I’m not explaining it well, but I felt sure they weren’t just arguing with me.\n\nIt felt like they were using the altar, and the parish committee people, to push me out… to make me disappear from the family’s place, almost, and make everyone forget what I’ve done for them. I’m sorry if that sounds strange. I’m just trying to answer honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel that others are acting against you or plotting to harm you, even when others don’t share that belief?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say “often” with everyone, doctor… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But with my in-laws, yes, I did feel that way.\n\nThat’s really why I came in. I knew they weren’t just arguing with me. It felt like they were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out… and make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family. I know that may sound like a lot, but that’s how it felt to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that people outside of your in-laws were also acting against you or trying to harm you in some way?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think it was really people outside the family, doctor. At least, not in the same way.\n\nIt was mostly my in-laws. I came in because I knew it wasn’t just normal arguing with me. It felt like they were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out, and to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family. I know that may sound dramatic… I’m sorry. But that’s what I believed was happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any beliefs or ideas that other people close to you say are not true, but you still feel certain about them even though there’s no clear proof?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose that would be the thing with my in-laws. I don’t want to sound unreasonable, doctor, and maybe other people didn’t see it the way I did, but I felt certain they weren’t just arguing with me.\n\nI believed they were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out, like they wanted to erase my place in the family and make everyone forget what I’ve done for them. I know there may not have been clear proof in the way you mean, but at the time it felt very true to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs that others were acting against you or that things had special meanings specifically for you, outside of what you experienced with your in-laws?\n\nPatient: Outside of my in-laws… no, not really, doctor. Not that I can think of.\n\nIt was that situation with them that felt so clear to me. I came in because I knew it wasn’t just a family disagreement — I felt they were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out, and to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family.\n\nI know that may sound… maybe too much. But that was the belief I had. I don’t think I had that kind of feeling about other people or other things having special meanings for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other beliefs that felt very real to you, even if others didn’t agree or there wasn’t clear proof, outside of the situation with your in-laws?\n\nPatient: No, doctor… not outside of that, I don’t think. I don’t think I was having those kinds of unusual beliefs then.\n\nThe voices and seeing my mother — that was during the time when I was barely sleeping and rushing around, when I felt so sure I had this special duty to fix the altar. I know that sounds strange now. But when my mood dropped afterward, I wasn’t hearing or seeing those things anymore. I was just… down, ashamed, and tired.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about your in-laws acting against you ever spread to feeling that others outside the family were also plotting against you, or did it stay focused only on them?\n\nPatient: It mostly stayed focused on them, doctor… my in-laws. Yes… I think so, doctor. I don’t know if I’d call it “elevated,” but there were days when I felt very bright, very sure of myself… like I knew exactly what was right.

I’m just trying to answer honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel that others are acting against you or plotting to harm you, even when others don’t share that belief?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say “often” with everyone, doctor… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But with my in-laws, yes, I did feel that way.\n\nThat’s really why I came in. I knew they weren’t just arguing with me. It felt like they were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out… and make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family. I know that may sound like a lot, but that’s how it felt to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that people outside of your in-laws were also acting against you or trying to harm you in some way?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think it was really people outside the family, doctor. At least, not in the same way.\n\nIt was mostly my in-laws. I came in because I knew it wasn’t just normal arguing with me. It felt like they were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out, and to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family. I know that may sound dramatic… I’m sorry. But that’s what I believed was happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any beliefs or ideas that other people close to you say are not true, but you still feel certain about them even though there’s no clear proof?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose that would be the thing with my in-laws. I don’t want to sound unreasonable, doctor, and maybe other people didn’t see it the way I did, but I felt certain they weren’t just arguing with me.\n\nI believed they were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out, like they wanted to erase my place in the family and make everyone forget what I’ve done for them. I know there may not have been clear proof in the way you mean, but at the time it felt very true to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs that others were acting against you or that things had special meanings specifically for you, outside of what you experienced with your in-laws?\n\nPatient: Outside of my in-laws… no, not really, doctor. Not that I can think of.\n\nIt was that situation with them that felt so clear to me. I came in because I knew it wasn’t just a family disagreement — I felt they were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out, and to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family.\n\nI know that may sound… maybe too much. But that was the belief I had. I don’t think I had that kind of feeling about other people or other things having special meanings for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other beliefs that felt very real to you, even if others didn’t agree or there wasn’t clear proof, outside of the situation with your in-laws?\n\nPatient: No, doctor… not outside of that, I don’t think. I know I’ve said it already, and I don’t want to keep repeating myself, but it was really about my in-laws.\n\nAt the time it felt very real that they weren’t just arguing with me. I felt they were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out, and to make people forget what I’ve done for this family. I understand that may sound like a lot… but that was the belief I had.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other thoughts or beliefs that felt very real to you, even if they weren’t shared by others or there wasn’t clear evidence, that you haven’t already mentioned?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there’s anything else I haven’t told you, doctor… at least not that comes to mind.\n\nIt really was that situation with my in-laws. I came in because I knew they weren’t just arguing with me — I felt they were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out, and to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family.\n\nI know maybe that sounds strong, and I don’t want you to think I’m trying to be difficult. But that was the thing that felt real to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any unusual thoughts or beliefs when you were feeling especially down or low, that others didn’t share, even if they seem strange to you now?\n\nPatient: When I was really low… I mostly just felt guilty and sad, doctor. I don’t think I was having those kinds of unusual beliefs then.\n\nThe voices and seeing my mother — that was during the time when I was barely sleeping and rushing around, when I felt so sure I had this special duty to fix the altar. I know that sounds strange now. But when my mood dropped afterward, I wasn’t hearing or seeing those things anymore. I was just… down, ashamed, and tired.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about your in-laws acting against you ever spread to feeling that others outside the family were also plotting against you, or did it stay focused only on them?\n\nPatient: It mostly stayed focused on them, doctor… my in-laws. I know that may sound harsh, but that was how it felt to me then.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other beliefs that felt very real to you, even if others didn’t agree or there wasn’t clear evidence, that were not related to your in-laws?\n\nPatient: Not really, doctor… I don’t think it was about other people, if that’s what you mean.\n\nThe main thing was my in-laws. I felt very sure they weren’t just disagreeing with me — that they were using the altar and the parish committee to push me aside, to make everyone forget what I had done for the family. That’s the belief that felt real to me then.\n\nI hope I’m answering that the right way.\n\nDoctor: After the time when you were hearing your mother’s voice and felt your in-laws were plotting against you, did you go through a period where you felt extremely down nearly every day, lost interest in things you usually enjoy, or had other signs of depression?\n\nPatient: Yes… after all that energy went out of me, I did feel very low. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it was, but most mornings I’d wake up with this heavy sadness, like there just wasn’t any joy left in the house… or in me.\n\nIs that the sort of thing you mean, doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt unusually energetic or elevated, where you needed less sleep, or where your thoughts were racing, even if it felt good at the time?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so, doctor. I don’t know if I’d call it “elevated,” but there were days when I felt very bright, very sure of myself… like I knew exactly what was right.\n\nAnd then, if someone questioned me about the altar, I would get upset quickly. I’d snap at them, almost like they were insulting my whole family, not just asking a question. I’m not proud of that. At the time it felt justified, but looking back… maybe it was more intense than it needed to be. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\n[Last Question]\nCan you describe how long those periods of feeling unusually energetic or very sure of yourself lasted, and whether they included feeling like you didn’t need much sleep or that your thoughts were moving very quickly?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 60 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Sleep_Disturbance\nmanifestation: \"At first I was sleeping only a couple of hours and still getting up to make lists, but now I lie awake before dawn worrying and never feel rested.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
It wasn’t just one bad night, doctor… at first, during that time, I was sleeping maybe only a couple of hours and then I’d still get up and start making lists, like there were things I had to organize right away. I didn’t feel as tired then as I probably should have.

Now it’s a bit different. I’m not up doing things so much, but I wake before dawn and just lie there worrying, and I don’t really feel rested after. I hope that answers what you’re asking.

I think… they were tied together, doctor. It didn’t feel like it came from nowhere exactly.

I was just… down, ashamed, and tired.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about your in-laws acting against you ever spread to feeling that others outside the family were also plotting against you, or did it stay focused only on them?\n\nPatient: It mostly stayed focused on them, doctor… my in-laws. I don’t think I was thinking the whole town or strangers were plotting or anything like that.\n\nIt was more that I felt they were using the altar and the parish committee against me, to push me out and make people forget what I’d done for the family. I know that may sound harsh, but that was how it felt to me then.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other beliefs that felt very real to you, even if others didn’t agree or there wasn’t clear evidence, that were not related to your in-laws?\n\nPatient: Not really, doctor… I don’t think it was about other people, if that’s what you mean.\n\nThe main thing was my in-laws. I felt very sure they weren’t just disagreeing with me — that they were using the altar and the parish committee to push me aside, to make everyone forget what I had done for the family. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it was, but most mornings I’d wake up with this heavy sadness, like there just wasn’t any joy left in the house… or in me.\n\nIs that the sort of thing you mean, doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt unusually energetic or elevated, where you needed less sleep, or where your thoughts were racing, even if it felt good at the time?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so, doctor.   },

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I think it was mostly around them, doctor… I don’t remember thinking every little thing had a meaning for me, if that’s what you mean.

But at the time it did feel like more than just a family argument. I came in because I really believed my in-laws were using the altar and even the parish committee to push me out, to make people forget what I had done for the family. I know that sounds… maybe too much, saying it now. But that was what I believed then. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?

I know there may not have been clear proof in the way you mean, but at the time it felt very true to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs that others were acting against you or that things had special meanings specifically for you, outside of what you experienced with your in-laws?\n\nPatient: Outside of my in-laws… no, not really, doctor. I felt they were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out, and to make people forget what I’ve done for this family. I understand that may sound like a lot… but that was the belief I had.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other thoughts or beliefs that felt very real to you, even if they weren’t shared by others or there wasn’t clear evidence, that you haven’t already mentioned?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there’s anything else I haven’t told you, doctor… at least not that comes to mind.\n\nIt really was that situation with my in-laws. I came in because I knew they weren’t just arguing with me — I felt they were using the altar and the parish committee to push me out, and to make everyone forget what I’ve done for this family.\n\nI know maybe that sounds strong, and I don’t want you to think I’m trying to be difficult. But that was the thing that felt real to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any unusual thoughts or beliefs when you were feeling especially down or low, that others didn’t share, even if they seem strange to you now?\n\nPatient: When I was really low… I mostly just felt guilty and sad, doctor. I don’t think I was having those kinds of unusual beliefs then.\n\nThe voices and seeing my mother — that was during the time when I was barely sleeping and rushing around, when I felt so sure I had this special duty to fix the altar. It felt connected to how I was then, I think. Not like it came from some completely separate place.
